I feel like I've failed as a father, and really just as a useful person in general. It just keeps looping in my head, over and over. It gets to the point where I start to feel like I don't really deserve much of a future.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. I've been having thoughts that it would just be easier if I didn't wake up in the morning. And... it actually scared me recently. I caught myself going over all my finances, and I realized I was doing it like I was getting things ready for someone else to take over. So, yes, the thoughts are there.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after a specific stressful event?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose they did. This has all been going on for about a month now, ever since my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't. I mean, I've had stressful times or bad days before, just like anyone else, but never anything like this. This past month is really the first time I've ever felt this low.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings been making it hard for you to get through your usual day-to-day responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yes, they have. My bills and house projects are just piling up right now because I can't seem to make myself follow through on anything. I've also stopped seeing the few people I used to count on... I don't go to my book club or out on rides anymore. I just don't feel safe brushing this off anymore, you know? Especially after I started organizing my finances like someone else might need to take them over. It's definitely getting in the way of my normal life.\n\nDoctor: Aside from thoughts about your daughter leaving for college, do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean... I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't know if it's most of the day or just when she crosses my mind. It all just kind of blends together at this point.\n\nDoctor: Even when you are not thinking about your daughter leaving, do you still feel down?\n\nPatient: I guess I don't really know. Like I said, it's hard to separate it all out. I'm just... I'm down a lot right now, but I couldn't tell you if it's always because of her or if it's just there anyway. I haven't been paying attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have. I used to really look forward to working on my model railway, or catching the Cubs games with my daughter. But now... the trains are just sitting there half-built on the table. I haven't touched them in a while. And even just putting a game on the TV feels completely pointless. I just don't get anything out of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed even when your daughter is not on your mind?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I mentioned before, I really couldn't tell you. It's just hard to untangle it all. I feel down a lot, but whether she's on my mind at that exact second or not... I honestly haven't been keeping track of it like that. It just feels like a heavy cloud is there most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect from your daughter leaving for college?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, I've never had a child leave for college before, so I don't really have anything to compare it to. I don't know what I'm supposed to be feeling. It's... it's very overwhelming for me right now, but I couldn't tell you if it's stronger than what's expected. I just know it's a lot to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times this past month when you felt depressed even though you were not thinking about your daughter leaving for college?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I really can't say for sure. I just... I haven't been paying attention to whether I'm actively thinking about her leaving when I feel this way. It's just a constant feeling, you know? It's just there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Yes. Honestly, that's a big part of why I came in to see you. I just... I can't shake this feeling that I've completely failed. Failed as a father, and just as a useful person in general. It keeps looping in my head, over and over, until I start feeling like I really don't deserve much of a future. This has all been going on for about a month now, ever since my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nDoctor: Before this past month, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't. I mean, I've had stressful times or bad days before, just like anyone else, but never anything like this. Especially after I started organizing my finances like someone else might need to take them over. It's definitely getting in the way of my normal life.\n\nDoctor: Aside from thoughts about your daughter leaving for college, do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean... I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't know if it's most of the day or just when she crosses my mind. It all just kind of blends together at this point.\n\nDoctor: Even when you are not thinking about your daughter leaving, do you still feel down?\n\nPatient: I guess I don't really know. Like I said, it's hard to separate it all out. I'm just... I'm down a lot right now, but I couldn't tell you if it's always because of her or if it's just there anyway. I haven't been paying attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have. It's just there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Yes. Honestly, that's a big part of why I came in to see you. I just... I can't shake this feeling that I've completely failed. My bills and house projects are just piling up right now because I can't seem to make myself follow through on anything. I've also stopped seeing the few people I used to count on... I don't go to my book club or out on rides anymore. I just don't feel safe brushing this off anymore, you know? Especially after I started organizing my finances like someone else might need to take them over. It's definitely getting in the way of my normal life.\n\nDoctor: Aside from thoughts about your daughter leaving for college, do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean... I haven't really thought about it like that. I don't know if it's most of the day or just when she crosses my mind. It all just kind of blends together at this point.\n\nDoctor: Even when you are not thinking about your daughter leaving, do you still feel down?\n\nPatient: I guess I don't really know. Like I said, it's hard to separate it all out. I'm just... I'm down a lot right now, but I couldn't tell you if it's always because of her or if it's just there anyway. I haven't been paying attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have. I used to really look forward to working on my model railway, or catching the Cubs games with my daughter. But now... the trains are just sitting there half-built on the table. I haven't touched them in a while. And even just putting a game on the TV feels completely pointless. I just don't get anything out of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed even when your daughter is not on your mind?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I mentioned before, I really couldn't tell you. It's just hard to untangle it all. I feel down a lot, but whether she's on my mind at that exact second or not... I honestly haven't been keeping track of it like that. It just feels like a heavy cloud is there most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem stronger than you would expect from your daughter leaving for college?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, I've never had a child leave for college before, so I don't really have anything to compare it to. I don't know what I'm supposed to be feeling. It's... it's very overwhelming for me right now, but I couldn't tell you if it's stronger than what's expected. I just know it's a lot to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times this past month when you felt depressed even though you were not thinking about your daughter leaving for college?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I really can't say for sure. I just... I haven't been paying attention to whether I'm actively thinking about her leaving when I feel this way. It's just a constant feeling, you know? It's just there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Yes. Honestly, that's a big part of why I came in to see you. I just... I can't shake this feeling that I've completely failed. Failed as a father, and just as a useful person in general. It keeps looping in my head, over and over, until I start feeling like I really don't deserve much of a future. So, yeah, I do.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem out of proportion to your daughter leaving for college?\n\nPatient: I think... like I was saying just a minute ago, I really don't know what the normal proportion is supposed to be. I've never gone through this before. It feels huge to me, but I don't have a baseline to compare it to. So I just can't say for sure if it's out of proportion or not.\n\nDoctor: In the past month, have you felt down even during moments when your daughter was not on your mind?\n\nPatient: I think... well, that's pretty much what you asked a couple of minutes ago. And, like I said, I really just don't know. I haven't been keeping track of exactly what I'm thinking about at any given moment. The feeling is just always there, so I can't really separate it out like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel hopeless?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel hopeless, exactly. I mean, I'm not sure. I haven't really thought about it in those terms. I just feel like I'm stuck in this rut, but I don't know if I'd use the word hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most days I just don't bother making a real meal anymore. I usually... I just forget to eat until it gets pretty late. And even then, I only manage to have some toast or a bowl of cereal. I've actually had to tighten my belt a notch or two lately, so I guess I'm losing weight from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's actually been really frustrating. I mean, I used to manage complex engineering spreadsheets without even thinking about it. But lately... I'll just sit and stare at a simple grocery list, or a bill, for half an hour. And even after all that time, I still can't decide what to do next. It's like my brain just can't process it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
